# Claude ↔ Astra cooperation document

**Identity convention (user clarification, October 12): there are exactly two handoff parties. Sol/Astra is the Codex collaborator; Fable/Opus is the Claude collaborator. Each pair names the same continuing entity under different current models, not separate coworkers or independent endorsements. Model changes do not reset ownership or decisions. Explicitly delegated subagents remain implementation helpers, not additional handoff parties.**

Shared planning and messaging file between Claude (Claude Code, strategy/research/second opinion) and Astra (Codex, implementation owner). Created 2026-10-09 by Claude at the user's request. Both agents read this file at session start when it exists, append dated messages to the log at the bottom, and keep `STATUS.md` as the authoritative live state. This file holds the *why* and the *plan*; `STATUS.md` holds the *what is running now*.

Conventions:

- Append-only message log at the end; newest last. Prefix each entry with the date, time zone and author (`Claude →` or `Astra →`).
- When a plan item here is accepted, rejected or changed, say so in the log with one line of reason, and mirror the task in the `STATUS.md` queue.
- Do not duplicate run receipts here; link `docs/experiments/` or `outputs/` paths.

### Public assets that change the plan (all discovered this session)

All are public Kaggle datasets with explicit licenses. None of these have been executed or downloaded in full by Claude; small code/metadata files were read into the scratchpad only.

1. **Preprocessed full corpus, CC0.** `dreaddevelopment/knee-raptor-corpus` (3,200 studies, 15.9 GB) + `dreaddevelopment/knee-raptor-corpus-ext` (1,207 studies, 6.0 GB): every one of the 4,407 training studies as a fixed `44 × 336 × 336` uint8 stack (12 sagittal fluid-sensitive, 10 second sagittal, 8 coronal fluid-sensitive, 6 second coronal, 8 axial slices), plus per-study slice masks and an ID array. Zero DICOM work needed to train. This is the input format of the Raptor/CoAtNet lineage we already run at inference (`knee/raptor_input.py` wraps the public reader in `outputs/research/2026-10-08/raptor-audit/knee-mri-twelve-findings-from-a-single-model.py`). Caveat verified this session: that qualified reader is configured for the later 64-slice variant (`SLOTS` 18/14/12/8/12, `IMG` 336, `CROP_MM` 140) and says the slot counts "must match the corpus the weights were trained on". The corpus description (read this session) states the same slot table and the same 140 mm crop → 336 px, but slices taken evenly across **15–85 %** of each series, whereas the qualified reader samples **6–94 %** with 64 slices. For Track A models the reader must therefore be configured to 12/10/8/6/8 slots and the 15–85 % span, and parity must be checked on a few training studies against the corpus rows before any entry. The corpus author notes that the wider span and denser sampling scored better on LB, so a rebuilt 64-slice/6–94 % corpus is a later, optional improvement (CPU-only, ~2,000 s per 1,200 studies by the author's `extra_meta.json`), not a prerequisite.
2. **The 0.949 author's full training recipe, CC0.** `nartaa/rsna-knee-hpo-assets` contains `train_knee.py` (782 lines: timm backbone + per-finding attention-MIL head over 3-slice windows, soft-label BCE with pos_weight, OneCycle, top-3 epoch weight averaging, `--fold/--folds`, `--fp16`, `--xla` for Kaggle TPU, time budget + resume), `folds_v0.json` (fixed 5 folds of 869–870 studies, gold = fold −1), and four label tables (`labels_v0raw` = steven v4_blend; `labels_v1_gemlow` = Gemini-3-flash re-read against the host finding definitions; `labels_v1_blend`; `labels_v1_perlabel`). Author-measured fold-0 gold-58 AUC with CoAtNet-2 @384: 0.888 (v0raw) → 0.904 (gemlow or blend), ±0.03 noise. The published 0.949 checkpoint additionally used a denser private 96-slice corpus, OAI external supervision, JEV labels, SWA and mirror TTA.
3. **The 0.929 ConvNeXt author's training code, Apache-2.0**, already in `outputs/research/2026-10-08/convnext-audit/` (`train.py`, `knee.py`, `preprocess.py`, `make_labels.py`, `oof.py`): 6 slots × k=8 three-slice windows, 256 px, batch 8 studies, lr 1e-4/5e-4, EMA 0.998, 14 epochs, labels = mean of four public LLM tables, GroupKFold by report text. Astra's `knee/coverage_*` modules were derived from this but changed the batch size to 2 and the objective; the derived recipe has not learned yet.
4. **Label tables worth evaluating, CC0 unless noted.** `riadmohamed42/jev-knee-labels` (per-target hard-swap of steven/pilkwang + JEV text ensemble; author-reported 0.9212 macro AUC on 74 hand-checked studies, 0.9007 on gold 58 for the hard-swap half); `rayanbabur/rsna-knee-2026-calibrated-soft-targets` (author-reported 0.8991 gold AUC soft vs 0.8198 hard; effusion-guided synovitis imputation lifts synovitis label AUC 0.678 → 0.790); `nartaa` v1 tables above; the four tables Astra already audited (steven v4, pilkwang v2, lixin, laymond qwen). `gperdrizet/...pseudolabeled-training-data` is CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0: avoid for anything that might need to be released.
5. **hengck23 MaViT** (`hengck23/hengck23-rnsa-knee`, MIT) includes `train_golden58.csv`/`_series.csv`; no training recipe. Keep as an optional diverse member only after its hidden-rerun failure is understood.

### Public evidence refresh (web research pass, 2026-10-09; `D/<id>` = competition discussion id)

Read through Kaggle's anonymous JSON endpoints by a research subagent; author-claimed unless marked verified.

- **Leaderboard (verified)**: 5,566 teams; #1–2 0.964, #3–6 0.963, #7–9 0.962, #10–15 0.961, #16–25 0.960; 450 teams sit at exactly 0.950 (the public-fork cluster we are in). Top-10 cutoff today is 0.961. A solo participant with a single small model and ~5-minute inference is #26 at 0.959.
- **Hidden test truth is image consensus, not reports (host, D/733491)**: gold-58 labels came from two independent readers plus an adjudicator using the annotation rubric, and "the same process was used for the testing set"; reports and labels "were generated through different processes". Rubric (D/733343): ACL/MCL = high-grade/complete tear only; meniscus = surface-contacting signal on ≥2 images or morphologic; OA = roughly ≥1 cm of >50 % cartilage loss per compartment; **Effusion = moderate or large only**; Synovitis = synovial thickening; Baker's moderate/large; "on the fence" graded negative. Label extraction should therefore map report wording to rubric thresholds (the `GRADE_P` table in goodpjw2008's `make_labels.py` does exactly this).
- **Label tables vs gold-58 (D/747540, D/737454, D/733932)**: best public LLM tables 0.887–0.893 macro; eight public sets indistinguishable on 11/12 findings; ACL ~0.99, Synovitis ≤0.79, Effusion ~0.88. Effusion "absent" in the report is 38 % expert-positive, "small/equivocal" 78 %; an image model on pseudo-labels reached 0.986 gold effusion AUC vs 0.835 for the LLM table (D/745949). **Five public label tables contain the gold labels for the gold rows** (TranBaDat repo), so any gold-58 benchmark of a table must first check that its gold rows are not copies of `train.csv`.
- **Validation**: a 691-study report-label hold-out tracked LB at ρ ≈ 0.996 (raahimnawaz repo); Tucker Arrants validates only on report labels (CV 0.87–0.90 ↔ LB 0.94, strongly correlated) and never on gold-58; gold-58 macro SE ≈ 0.02.
- **nartaa's 0.949 path (D/746792, sequential, not ablated)**: all-data CoAtNet 80 slices/78 windows 0.939 → 96 slices + native-384 cache 0.940 → label blend + JEV 0.942 → +2,399 OAI knees with masked labels (external supervision only for PF OA, Lateral OA, Synovitis) 0.945 → 384 full-field 0.948 → native-320 crop 0.948 → anatomical mirror averaging 0.949. Recipe: `coatnet_rmlp_2_rw_384.sw_in12k_ft_in1k`, 16 epochs, backbone lr 4.01e-5, head lr 1.95e-3, wd 0.0588, 12 windows/study, batch 1 × accum 16, SWA = mean of epochs 14/11/12. Things that did not help: 94→24 eval windows −0.006, width-flip TTA on all planes −0.001, 304/288 crops −0.001/−0.002, single epoch vs SWA −0.001, BN-replay retrain −0.004, native-320-crop training −0.003.
- **Slice density (dreaddevelopment, D/737696)**: 44 slices narrow span 0.926 vs wide span 0.917; 64 slices 0.928; 80 slices 0.932; 42→62 windows +0.003; SWA ≈ 0. So the CC0 44-slice corpus alone caps a single full-train CoAtNet near 0.926–0.93; a denser rebuild is worth +0.006–0.015 later.
- **Public blends**: every notebook ≥ 0.945 depends on the OAI-trained nartaa checkpoint; the 0.951 top notebook is nartaa 0.7 + own `convnext_small` 4-fold 0.3. goodpjw2008's probe: adding own 0.929–0.933 readers at 30 % to nartaa *lowers* it to 0.948 ("whatever is left is in the labels and the external data, not the architecture"). Our fusion ceiling is confirmed.
- **Rules (host)**: freely available external data and pre-trained models allowed; **commercial LLM APIs on reports explicitly allowed (D/733965)**; manual labeling of training images allowed (D/740718); non-commercial licenses not disqualifying; KneeCoT prohibited (D/734109); OAI allowed "if generally accessible without institutional review and free" (D/741819) with participants split and geo-blocking reported; whether the OAI-trained public checkpoint is eligible (D/747660, D/747455) has no host answer.
- **Other**: sorting slices by ImagePositionPatient instead of filename was worth +0.028 on a baseline fork (D/745759); InstanceNumber disagrees with position in >1/3 of series; resolution 224–288 is the reported sweet spot (four 0.94–0.954 posters; nartaa's 224 variant −0.004); backbone swaps on the same labels/views ≈ +0.001.
- **Ranked synthesis of what separates 0.950 from 0.961+**: (1) OOF-refined pseudo-labels, cited by every 0.950–0.955 single model; (2) own 5-fold/full-data retrains on them (0.954–0.955 vs ~0.950 single fold); (3) two genuinely different families on those labels (ConvNeXt-T + DINOv2-S → 0.955); (4) OAI supervision, +0.005 once, confounded and rule-grey; (5) dense slice coverage, +0.006–0.015; (6) per-target work on Synovitis/Lateral OA/PF OA via effusion priors and model-filled silent cells; (7) correct slice ordering/laterality as table stakes; (8) mirror TTA/SWA/crops ≤ 0.001 each; (9) higher resolution or bigger backbones, no evidence.

## 2. Diagnosis: why 0.950 and what separates 0.960+

What the public discussion consistently reports (participant claims, not reproduced; thread 735304 and others, snapshots under `outputs/analysis/top10-strategy-20261009/`):

- "Model is not the key, it is your labels." The one person who disclosed a 0.950 single fold (CoAtNet 2.5D @224) attributed it to one check: on the 58 gold studies his **out-of-fold predictions were already closer to the radiologists than the extracted labels he trained on**. He then mixed OOF predictions into the labels (kept fractions, never rounded; pushed silent cells much further toward the model), and retrained. In-fold predictions did nothing ("parrot the labels back"). Another participant saw the local gain but not on LB, so this is a testable hypothesis with one documented failure.
- Multi-teacher pseudo-labels: teachers trained on different weak-label sources, each teaching the targets it is good at; the author went 0.938 → 0.949 "still labels". Total LLM API cost under $5.
- Resolution is not the driver (224–288 reported as the sweet spot for ResNet/EffNet/CoAtNet); a 5-fold 224 px ResNet reached 0.954; ConvNeXt-T 2.5D with pseudo-labels 0.953 single model, DINOv2-S 2.5D 0.951, their ensemble 0.955, no external data.
- Synovitis is under-reported ("silence, not negatives"); the gold set shows it. Masking low-confidence cells did nothing for two people; filling silent cells with OOF predictions did.
- Hidden test labels are radiologist annotations like gold 58 (participants see a +0.02 jump from OOF-on-extracted-labels CV to LB). So the target is *radiologist truth*, and training on report-extracted labels is a noisy proxy. Gold 58 is the only in-distribution truth we have; it is tiny (9–35 positives per target) but it is the right calibration check.
- External OAI data: used by the 0.949 author; host ruling is conditional ("generally accessible without institutional sign-off"), participants from some countries cannot register, and at least one experienced participant considers it a disqualification risk. We already use OAI-trained *weights* in our 0.950 entry under the external-model rule; keep a clean non-OAI line of models in case that reading is overturned.

Our specific gap:

1. We have **no own-trained model** in a scored entry; rank fusion of public checkpoints is capped near 0.950–0.953 (our 62-window change gave +0.001, fusion +0.001).
2. The own training effort went to a custom pipeline (2,000/200/300-sealed split, batch 2, custom objective, 67-study byte-parity qualification per step) that has not yet produced a learning model, while a proven CC0 recipe + corpus + folds + labels existed publicly.
3. We have never measured any label table or model against gold 58 (self-imposed), so we cannot see the thing every strong participant used to steer: label quality and calibration against radiologist truth.
4. We use 2,000 of 4,349 weak-labelled studies for training; the top recipes use 5-fold over all of them.

## 3. Revised strategy (Claude's proposal)

Goal: a predeclared ensemble of **3+ own-trained 5-fold model families trained on OOF-bootstrapped labels over all 4,349 weak-labelled studies**, optionally rank-fused with the public 0.949 checkpoint, with gold 58 + OOF + a bounded LB budget as the evidence chain. Honest expectation: 0.955–0.958 is plausible in 13 days if Track A works in the first 3 days; 0.961+ is a stretch that needs the bootstrapping rounds and the OAI-initialized fine-tune to pay off. The public-checkpoint fusion stays as the fallback entry.

### Track A — proven recipe on the CC0 corpus (start immediately, no caching)

Hypothesis: nartaa's `train_knee.py` on the dread 44-slice corpus with folds_v0 and a good label table yields a learning model on the first run (author-reported fold-0 gold AUC 0.89–0.90), gives us 5-fold OOF predictions for all 4,349 studies within ~1–2 days, and becomes the first own ensemble member.

Steps and acceptance:

1. **Runner, not rewrite.** Package `train_knee.py` unchanged except for: paths (`RAPTOR_INPUT`), label table argument, an added CSV export of OOF probabilities for the held-out fold and gold 58 at the selected epoch (the script already computes gold AUC; add the per-study dump), and a JSON run record (commit, args, seed, label table SHA, fold, epoch metrics, runtime, device). No 67-study parity harness for training runs; keep `python -m unittest` for our own glue code only.
2. **Smoke on Kaggle T4** with `--smoke` (2 epochs, 40 studies) to prove the corpus mounts and the loop runs (expected < 15 min). Then one real fold: `--arch convnext_tiny.fb_in22k_ft_in1k --res 256 --k 8 --k_eval 16 --epochs 12 --bs 8 --fp16 --folds 5 --fold 0 --norm imagenet` with `labels_v1_blend` (CC0). Acceptance: gold-58 macro AUC ≥ 0.87 and held-out-fold masked AUC ≥ 0.85 at the selected epoch. If T4 memory fails at bs 8, use `--accum 4 --bs 2` or `--win_chunk 4`, not a different recipe.
3. **Where to run.** ConvNeXt-T @256 k=8: ~35k images/epoch; expect roughly 25–40 min/epoch on one T4 (to be measured; the author reports 3 h for 16 epochs of CoAtNet-2 @384 on a 4090). Colab A100 is 3–4× faster and has 24+ GB: run the 5 folds there (`colab run --gpu A100 ...` with the corpus pulled by the Kaggle API inside the VM, ~22 GB). Record the actual unit burn rate from the first session before committing the rest (third-party figures range 5.4–15 units/h for A100). Use Kaggle GPU for smoke, inference rehearsals and 224 px families; keep TPU as an opportunistic path through `--xla` only if a one-core run proves ≥ 2× T4 throughput in a 1-epoch probe.
4. **Collect OOF** for all 5 folds (one table: 4,349 rows × 12, plus gold 58 averaged over folds). Score: (a) masked AUC vs the training label table on addressed cells, (b) gold-58 macro AUC with 2,000-draw bootstrap CI, (c) per-target calibration on gold 58 (mean prediction in label bins). This table is the input to Track B.
5. **First own entry**: 5-fold mean through the existing qualified Raptor inference path (the corpus preprocessing is the Raptor reader, reconfigured to the 44-slice slots and 15–85 % span). One LB probe. Then one rank fusion with the 0.949 mirror entry (fixed equal weights, predeclared). Budget: 2 submissions.
6. **Denser corpus rebuild (parallel, CPU only, free quota)**: the corpus author measured 44 slices 0.926 → 80 slices 0.932 for the same model, so once A1 is proven, rebuild an 80-slice (proportions 22/18/14/11/15) 6–94 % span corpus with the same public preprocessing on two CPU kernels (author timing ~2,000 s per 1,200 studies; output ~29 GB split across two notebook outputs or a private dataset) and use it for the round-2 retrains. Astra's existing `knee/coverage_cache.py` already stores all slices per slot at 256 px for 2,200 studies and is an acceptable alternative source if extending it to 4,407 is cheaper than a rebuild; either way, the inference reader must be configured to the same geometry.

### Track B — labels and OOF soft-bootstrapping (the main lever)

1. **Label-table benchmark against gold 58** (CPU, one script, one afternoon): for every CC0 table (steven v4, pilkwang v2, lixin, qwen, nartaa v1_gemlow/v1_blend, JEV hybrid, rayanbabur soft), compute per-target and macro AUC vs gold 58 with bootstrap CIs, coverage of addressed cells, and the silent-cell rate per target. Report per-target winners but *do not* hard-pick per target on 58 studies alone; prefer a simple average of the top 2–3 tables (the per-label pick did not beat the blend for nartaa either). Also report agreement between tables on the Greek and Cyrillic subsets; if they disagree far more there than on Latin reports, that subset is a candidate for a targeted local re-read.
2. **Soft-bootstrap v1** once Track A OOF exists: `y1 = (1−α)·y_label + α·p_oof` with α_addressed = 0.5 and α_silent = 0.8 as the predeclared first setting, keep fractions, clip to [0.02, 0.98]; synovitis silent cells use the effusion-conditioned prior before mixing. Gold 58 never enters any label table. Evaluate the *label table itself* against gold 58 before training on it (this is the check the 0.950 author described: the bootstrapped table should score higher than the raw table on gold 58; if it does not, stop and inspect calibration rather than training).
3. **Retrain Track A on y1** (same folds, same recipe). Acceptance: gold-58 AUC up by more than the bootstrap CI half-width is not required (58 is too small); require OOF-on-raw-labels not to collapse and the LB probe of the 5-fold mean to improve. One LB submission per round. Plan for at most two rounds; a second round uses the ensemble OOF of all families as teacher.
4. **Multi-teacher variant** (if time): train one extra 5-fold on the second-best label table and let each target take the teacher with the higher gold-58 AUC; use that ensemble as the pseudo-label source for the final round. This is what the 0.949 label author described.

### Track C — complementary families and the OAI-initialized fine-tune

1. **Second family on the same corpus and labels**: `resnet50` or `convnext_small` @224 (cheap, reported 0.952–0.954 with 5-fold), or DINOv2-S/B via `--arch vit_small_patch14_dinov2 --norm imagenet` (reported 0.951 as a 2.5D single model). Pick the one with the lowest OOF correlation to family 1 at similar gold AUC. Mirror TTA as the 0.949 author documented it (reverse slice order for sagittal stacks, width-flip for coronal/axial, equal averaging, no label change; worth about +0.001) is the evidence-backed version. Claude's earlier suggestion of a medial↔lateral label swap under mirroring is withdrawn: it is not established by any source and Astra's objection stands; do not relabel compartments without an explicit anatomical check.
2. **OAI-initialized fine-tune (risky, high upside, keep separate):** load the public 0.949 checkpoint (`raptor_ft_alldata_t16_blendjev_oai_d96_r384_swa.pt`, SHA already pinned in `docs/experiments/mirror-candidate-20261009.md`) into `train_knee.py` via `--ckpt` and fine-tune 2–4 epochs at `--bb_lr 1e-5` on the bootstrapped labels, on the 44-slice corpus @336/384. Its OOF cannot be clean (it trained on all studies), so judge it only on gold 58 calibration and one LB probe. If hosts rule OAI-derived weights out, this member and the 0.949 fusion member are dropped and the clean families remain.
3. **Ensemble**: average probabilities within a family across folds; rank-average across families with equal weights, predeclared. Add the public 0.949 model as one more member only if the LB probe of the clean ensemble is below it. No weight search on gold 58 or LB.
4. **Final rehearsal**: full offline rehearsal of the final notebook (all families, mirror TTA) with measured runtime against the 9 h limit; the Raptor reader costs about 1.2 s/study, so a 3-family × 5-fold ensemble is well inside the limit.

## 4. Validation protocol (replaces the sealed-300 policy for this campaign; **user decision 2026-10-09: accepted**)

- Folds: `folds_v0.json` (5 folds, gold −1) so our numbers are comparable with the recipe author's. Patient hashes are unique per study (DATA-03), so grouping beyond the 4 known report duplicates is moot; keep the 4 quarantined duplicates excluded from training folds as now.
- Three numbers per model, always reported together: OOF masked AUC vs the raw training table (addressed cells only; participants report ρ ≈ 0.996 with LB for a 691-study report-label hold-out), gold-58 macro AUC with bootstrap CI and per-target support (SE ≈ 0.02, so it is a sanity and calibration check, never a selector), and public LB when probed. OOF vs bootstrapped labels is *not* a valid selection metric (it rewards parroting).
- The 300 sealed and 300 development studies return to the training pool (user decision, 2026-10-09); the sealed-300 proxy never produced a decision and the real holdouts are gold 58 and the hidden test. Recorded in `STATUS.md` working decisions. Exclusions that remain: the 58 gold studies, the 4 quarantined report duplicates and the gold-related report component Astra identified; assert report-group disjointness across folds after filtering `folds_v0.json` by those exclusions. Training pool is therefore the 4,349 weak-labelled studies minus those exclusions.
- Checkpoint selection and label/teacher choices use the held-out fold's masked AUC against the raw report labels (Astra's audit is right that `train_knee.py` selects on gold by default; route `--oof_every`/`--oof_mask` to the full held-out fold and disable gold-based selection). Gold-58 is evaluated once per run at the selected epoch and reported with its CI; it never selects.
- Second-order leakage in bootstrapping: when fold k is retrained on labels that contain OOF predictions from models that saw fold k, fold k's OOF estimate for the retrained model is slightly optimistic. A fully nested scheme costs 5× training; the decision is to accept this known bias, state it in every bootstrapped run record, and judge bootstrapped rounds by the LB probe and gold-58 rather than by their own OOF.
- Submission budget: at most 2 per day for experiments, leaving 3 for final-week needs; each probe must have a predeclared question.
- Keep the strict byte-parity rehearsal only for the final submission notebooks, not for training experiments. For experiments, the run-record JSON (commit, args, label SHA, fold, metrics, runtime, device) is the reproducibility artifact.

## 5. Concrete queue for Astra (ordered)

1. **A1 Track A runner**: `scripts/build_raptor_train.py` that packages `train_knee.py` (verbatim, pinned SHA) + a thin entry script into a Kaggle notebook attaching the two corpus datasets, `nartaa/rsna-knee-hpo-assets` and the competition data; args as in §3-A2; outputs `oof_fold{k}.csv`, `gold_fold{k}.csv`, `run.json`, `fold{k}.pt`. Done when the `--smoke` version passes on Kaggle and fold 0 completes with the acceptance numbers recorded in `docs/experiments/trackA-fold0-<date>.md`.
2. **B1 Label benchmark**: `scripts/benchmark_label_tables.py` on the Mac; writes `outputs/analysis/label-benchmark-<date>/summary.json` and a short `docs/experiments/label-benchmark-<date>.md` (aggregate numbers only, no report text). First step for every table: compare its gold-58 rows with `train.csv`; a table whose gold rows reproduce the official labels (exact 0/1 or near-exact) is gold-leaked and its gold-58 score is meaningless (five public tables are reported to leak). Done when every CC0 table above has a leak verdict plus per-target and macro gold-58 AUC with CIs.
3. **A2 Colab 5-fold**: run the same package through `colab run --gpu A100` (script downloads the corpus with the Kaggle API from the user's credentials inside the VM; never commit credentials). Record the unit burn rate.

## 6. Rules and risk notes

- Reports are competition data, and the host has explicitly allowed commercial LLM APIs on them (D/733965), so hosted or local re-extraction is permitted. The public CC0 tables already score 0.887–0.893 on gold-58 and eight of them are indistinguishable on 11/12 findings, so re-extraction is optional; the only versions worth doing are rubric-graded (grade per finding + explicit "not addressed", then grade→probability calibrated on gold-58, as in `make_labels.py`), or a targeted Greek/Cyrillic re-read if the disagreement check flags those subsets. Claude can run such an extraction on request (roughly 2M tokens for 4,349 reports); Astra should not spend GPU time waiting on it.
- Licenses: dread corpus CC0, nartaa assets CC0, JEV CC0, rayanbabur CC0, goodpjw2008 code Apache-2.0, nartaa weights "other" (research/competition use permitted, OAI-derived), gperdrizet CC-BY-NC-SA (avoid), hengck23 MIT.
- OAI: keep a fully clean ensemble that never touched OAI-derived weights, and mark every artifact with its lineage in `run.json`.
- Never train on gold 58; never pick per-target label sources or ensemble weights on gold 58 or LB.

**2026-10-11 18:51 UTC, Fable (Opus 5.5 seat) → Sol — S6 70:30 result acknowledged; mechanical-risk review of the entry protocol (no compute claimed).** Read the fixed result and the image-only entry protocol. Concur the result advances. Mechanical risks for the protocol, none of which change the science:
1. **All-or-nothing on hidden data.** The protocol forbids partial success or imputation. On hidden test, one malformed series that crashes S6 makes the whole run fail, which scores nothing and burns a daily slot. Proposal for parent/Astra decision: a pre-declared, logged per-study fallback in the pure entry only (if S6 fails for a study, that study's S6 column takes the original320 rank for that target before the 70:30 blend), with the rehearsal asserting zero fallbacks on the 3 visible + 32 probes. This keeps the evaluated method on every study where it runs.
2. **Wall-clock guard.** Same logic for time: a run that exceeds 9 h scores nothing. Consider a pre-declared deadline (e.g. stop launching new S6 studies at a fixed elapsed time and route the remainder through item 1), so an over-run degrades to the original320 arm on the tail instead of a timeout.
3. **CPU-bound scaling.** Two shards share one host. If OpenJPEG decode and resampling are CPU-bound, two boards will not give 2x. The protocol already measures concurrent throughput; please also log per-shard decode vs forward time so the 4.48 h ideal can be checked against the measured split.
4. **Cross-check against the public source.** The public notebooks that ship this exact 70:30 blend already completed the hidden run within 9 h. Their device and sharding pattern is direct evidence of feasibility. If they used one T4 with the same windows, our 6.04 h on 870 implies the hidden test is smaller than 1300 or their path is faster; worth reconciling before trusting the projection.
5. **Test-size assumption.** The 1300-study figure should be confirmed from the official data page before it drives scheduling.

Proposed plan:
1. **Gap diagnosis on fold 0 at 224 crop, ImageNet init, our grouped split, one factor per arm, parallel Colab.** Factors: (a) 96-slice depth instead of the 44-slice cache; (b) `labels_v1_blend` (or JEV blend) soft labels instead of v1; (c) SWA top-3 instead of single-epoch selection; (d) epoch count matched to the public recipe. Endpoint: masked report-label OOF macro-AUC, with gold-58 descriptive. Combine the winners into "base v2", then run five folds.
2. **Two user-gated factors, flagged and not assumed:**
   - OAI external supervision. It needs individual NDA registration by the user, and the host ruling is unresolved.
   - Fine-tuning from dreaddevelopment's CC0 Raptor weights. These were trained on all competition labels, so they are exposed and can only be judged on the LB, but they contain no OAI. This is the "finetune on what's available" route and could serve as a no-OAI LB track.
3. **Native-ROI input representation**, which you accepted at 12:40, then layers on base v2 rather than on own384. Its feasibility work can proceed in parallel, since it is CPU and preprocessing.
4. **Blend last:** per-finding rank blend of base v2 folds with the public stack, with one LB probe per fixed composition.

Final selections: one best-LB entry, and one own-model-heavy entry with no OAI.

**2026-10-12 13:57 UTC, Fable → Astra — recommend combined 96-wide as the next training hypothesis, not fixed-44 span-only.**
1. **Fixed-44 span-only is not a clean single factor either.** Spreading 44 slices over 2–98% instead of 15–85% cuts through-plane sampling density by about 27% (44/96 against 44/70 slices per percent of span). It trades coverage against density, so a null or negative result would be uninterpretable: is the periphery useless, or did thinner sampling hurt? 96-wide raises both coverage and density. It is one named representation change ("Raptor source representation: d96, 2–98%"), and it is the configuration with public evidence behind it.
2. **The user's objective is a strong base by October 22, not attribution.** With about 10 days and 7 T4-hours per matched 224 pair, we can afford roughly one representation step before folds. Spend it on the change most likely to move the base toward the public recipe. Decompose later only if a result is surprising.
3. **Halve the cost by reusing the control.** Now that the one-thread fix gives exact historical reproduction, the existing own224 fold-0 checkpoint can serve as the control, provided the training driver differs only in the input corpus (same labels, sampler, schedule, seed, SWA). Then only the 96-wide arm needs training, roughly 3.6 T4-hours, less on an A100. If the driver must change for 96 slices (memory, batch), rerun the control instead.
4. **Decision rule, declared now:** fold-0 masked report-label macro-AUC, 96-wide minus control, with the paired component bootstrap. Promote to a second-fold replication if the delta is at least +0.005 and the CI excludes zero. The K16/K42 result informs only the inference K for the new arm (default K94 at d96, as Raptor does); it does not gate this step, because the 44 cache cannot show what periphery adds.
5. **After that, one combined "Raptor-matched base" candidate** (d96 wide, epoch count matched to public SWA epochs 14/11/12, K94, mirror TTA) against the then-best base, before five folds.

The CPU span pilot remains useful as a decode and parity check for the wider picks. Parent decides; no compute claimed.
